26	| Project | `gen-lang-client-0306026826` (billing **Khelsutra Billing** `01420D-419EE0-53D83C`) | — | n/a (shared Gemini project) |
27	| GCS bucket | `gs://khelsutra-rally-corpus` (asia-south1, UBLA) | ~$0.02/GB/mo storage | `gcloud storage rm -r` (⚠ data loss) |
28	| Service account | `rally-worker@gen-lang-client-0306026826.iam.gserviceaccount.com` (`roles/storage.objectAdmin`) | $0 | `gcloud iam service-accounts delete` |
29	| SA key | `~/.gcp/rally-worker-sa.json` (local only, **never** committed) | $0 | delete the file + the key |
30	| Budget alert | `rally-100usd-guard` — $100, alerts at 50/90/100% | $0 | `gcloud billing budgets delete` |
31	| **GPU VM** | **NOT created** — blocked on `GPUS_ALL_REGIONS` quota (see §2) | **~$0.35/hr (T4) running, ~$0.01/hr stopped** | `teardown.sh` (stop or delete) |
32	| APIs enabled | serviceusage, compute, cloudbilling, billingbudgets, iam, cloudresourcemanager | $0 | leave on (free) |
33	
34	Run `bash deploy/gcp/teardown.sh` (see §6) to stop/delete the billable bits between sessions.
35	
36	---
37	
38	## 1. One-time project bootstrap (owner, console — already done 2026-06-20)
39	
40	A bare Gemini/AI-Studio project has the **Service Usage API disabled**, which blocks *all* gcloud
41	provisioning (gcloud's own `services enable` needs that API → chicken-and-egg). These were enabled
42	in the console; recorded here so a fresh project can be bootstrapped the same way:
43	
44	1. **Service Usage API** — https://console.developers.google.com/apis/api/serviceusage.googleapis.com/overview?project=gen-lang-client-0306026826
45	2. **Link a billing account** (a payment method) — https://console.cloud.google.com/billing/linkedaccount?project=gen-lang-client-0306026826
46	3. After (1), the rest enable via gcloud (done): `gcloud services enable compute.googleapis.com cloudbilling.googleapis.com billingbudgets.googleapis.com iam.googleapis.com cloudresourcemanager.googleapis.com`
47	
48	`provision.sh` (§5) is idempotent and re-runs all of step (3) + the bucket/SA/budget.
49	
50	---
51	
52	## 2. ⛔ GPU quota — the one remaining owner action
53	
54	New GCP projects start at **`GPUS_ALL_REGIONS` = 0**, the *global* cap that gates GPU creation even
55	when a regional per-type quota is non-zero. Confirmed 2026-06-20:
56	
57	```
58	asia-south1  NVIDIA_T4_GPUS = 1   NVIDIA_L4_GPUS = 1   (regional: OK)
59	GLOBAL       GPUS_ALL_REGIONS = 0  ← BLOCKS the VM
60	```
61	
62	**Owner:** IAM & Admin → **Quotas** → filter **"GPUs (all regions)"** → Edit → request **1** → submit.
63	(For a single T4 this is usually approved in minutes; sometimes a short review.) Re-check:
64	
65	```bash
66	gcloud compute project-info describe --project gen-lang-client-0306026826 \
67	  --flatten="quotas[]" --format="table(quotas.metric,quotas.limit)" | grep GPUS_ALL_REGIONS
68	```
69	
70	When that reads `1`, run §3.
71	
72	> **⚠ Spot does NOT skip the quota.** TESTED 2026-06-20: a **Spot** T4 create also fails with
73	> `Quota 'GPUS_ALL_REGIONS' exceeded. Limit: 0.0 globally` — `GPUS_ALL_REGIONS` gates **both**
74	> on-demand AND preemptible GPUs, so the increase above is required either way. (A pending request
75	> was filed via the Cloud Quotas API: `POST …/quotaPreferences` `GPUS-ALL-REGIONS-per-project`
76	> → 1.) **Note (2026-06-20, ADR-013 supersedes the ADR-011 split): GCP is the SOLE compute stack —
77	> BOTH training and serving run on GCP; DigitalOcean is dropped for compute.** Spot is still cheaper
78	> once the quota is granted, and the resumable cache makes preemption recoverable.
79	
80	---
81	
82	## 3. Create the GPU VM (after quota ≥ 1)
83	
84	T4 is plenty for WASB + Gen-0. A local SSD gives a fast `/scratch`. *(The native runner defaults to
85	**streaming** — it decodes frames in-process, skipping the slow cv2 PNG extraction; bit-identical to disk,
86	verified on a real GPU 2026-06-20. Set `indexing.wasb.stream_video=false` only if a container/codec cv2
87	can't seek; `WASB_SCRATCH` still backs the disk path.)*
88	
89	```bash
90	gcloud compute instances create rally-gpu \
91	  --project=gen-lang-client-0306026826 \
92	  --zone=asia-south1-a \
93	  --machine-type=n1-standard-8 \
94	  --accelerator=type=nvidia-tesla-t4,count=1 \
95	  --maintenance-policy=TERMINATE --provisioning-model=STANDARD \
96	  --image-family=ubuntu-2404-lts-amd64 --image-project=ubuntu-os-cloud \
97	  --boot-disk-size=120GB --boot-disk-type=pd-balanced \
98	  --local-ssd=interface=NVME \
99	  --service-account=rally-worker@gen-lang-client-0306026826.iam.gserviceaccount.com \
100	  --scopes=cloud-platform
101	```
102	
103	**Spot variant (cheaper; still needs the §2 quota — Spot is NOT a quota bypass):** same command with
104	`--provisioning-model=SPOT` (replacing `STANDARD`). Add `--instance-termination-action=STOP` to keep
105	the disk on preemption so a re-run resumes. Cheaper, can be preempted.
106	
107	> **Validated image (2026-06-20):** `--image-family=common-cu129-ubuntu-2204-nvidia-580
108	> --image-project=deeplearning-platform-release` ships the NVIDIA driver (no install-on-boot wait;
109	> `nvidia-smi` works immediately) — used for the first real L4 run. `gpu_setup.sh` installs miniconda
110	> on it. **T4 was capacity-stocked-out in `asia-south1-a`; an L4 (`g2-standard-4`, no `--accelerator`
111	> flag — L4 is built into g2) in `asia-south1-c` landed** — try L4/other zones if T4 is unavailable.
112	
113	### 3b. Observability — Ops Agent (GPU graphs + logs in the console)
114	The VM **Observability → GPU** graphs + Logs need the **Ops Agent** (the console's OS-policy install is
115	blocked on DLVM images). Install it directly + grant the worker SA the write roles (it otherwise only has
116	storage), then GPU/NVML metrics + syslog flow to Cloud Monitoring/Logging (tab populates in ~2-3 min):
117	```bash
118	P=gen-lang-client-0306026826; SA=rally-worker@$P.iam.gserviceaccount.com
119	# 1. ENABLE the APIs (bare Gemini project has them OFF — the agent installs but its exports get
120	#    PermissionDenied/SERVICE_DISABLED until these are on; THIS was the empty-graphs cause 2026-06-20):
121	gcloud services enable logging.googleapis.com monitoring.googleapis.com --project $P
122	# 2. let rally-worker publish metrics + logs:
123	gcloud projects add-iam-policy-binding $P --member="serviceAccount:$SA" --role=roles/monitoring.metricWriter --condition=None
124	gcloud projects add-iam-policy-binding $P --member="serviceAccount:$SA" --role=roles/logging.logWriter   --condition=None
125	# 3. on the box (created with --scopes=cloud-platform):
126	curl -sSO https://dl.google.com/cloudagents/add-google-cloud-ops-agent-repo.sh && sudo bash add-google-cloud-ops-agent-repo.sh --also-install
127	# (if the agent was installed BEFORE the APIs were enabled: `sudo systemctl restart google-cloud-ops-agent`)
128	```
129	> Best: bake `curl …add-google-cloud-ops-agent-repo.sh|bash --also-install` into the VM
130	> `--metadata=startup-script` so observability is automatic on every box.
131	
132	> **Long remote steps must be detached.** A plain `ssh … "<long cmd>"` dies if the SSH drops (seen
133	> mid-build, 2026-06-20) — run them `nohup`'d on the box (`nohup bash setup.sh >log 2>&1 &`) and poll
134	> the log, so a dropped connection can't kill the install.
135	
136	> If `asia-south1-a` reports no T4 capacity, try `-b` / `-c`. The Ubuntu image needs the NVIDIA
137	> driver: either add `--metadata=install-nvidia-driver=True` style startup, or after SSH run GCP's
138	> driver installer, **or** use a Deep-Learning-VM image (`--image-family=common-cu124-ubuntu-2204`
139	> `--image-project=deeplearning-platform-release`) which ships the driver. Confirm `nvidia-smi` works
140	> before §4.
141	
142	SSH: `gcloud compute ssh rally-gpu --zone=asia-south1-a`.
143	
144	---
145	
146	## 4. Port the repo + WASB env onto the box
147	
148	The `deploy/digitalocean/` scripts are **provider-agnostic** (they detect the local NVMe / install
149	torch); reuse them on GCP:
150	
151	```bash
152	# from your machine — ship the repo, BAKING the source commit so the trained bundle is
153	# provenance-stamped. A git-archive tarball carries no .git, so training/gen0/harness.py would
154	# otherwise record git_sha:"unknown" (seen on the 2026-06-20 L4 run). It reads, in order:
155	# live `git rev-parse` → $RALLY_GIT_SHA → a GIT_SHA file at the repo root → "unknown".
156	git rev-parse HEAD > /tmp/GIT_SHA
157	git archive --format=tar.gz --add-file=/tmp/GIT_SHA -o /tmp/rally.tgz HEAD   # GIT_SHA → archive root (git ≥2.38)
158	gcloud compute scp /tmp/rally.tgz rally-gpu:/root/ --zone=asia-south1-a
159	gcloud compute ssh rally-gpu --zone=asia-south1-a -- 'mkdir -p ~/rally && tar -xzf ~/rally.tgz -C ~/rally'
160	# (Alternative, if not baking the file: `export RALLY_GIT_SHA=$(git rev-parse HEAD)` on the box
161	#  in the same shell that runs `python -m backend.worker train …`.)
162	
163	# on the box:
164	cd ~/rally
165	sudo bash deploy/digitalocean/mount_scratch.sh && export TMPDIR=/scratch   # detects the local NVMe
166	./deploy/digitalocean/bootstrap.sh --gpu                                   # venv + torch cu124 (confirm cuda True)
167	. .venv/bin/activate && pip install -e .[storage-gcs]                      # GCS backend
168	bash deploy/digitalocean/gpu_setup.sh                                      # native `wasb` conda env + WASB-SBDT
169	
170	# secrets (NOT in repo): the GCS SA JSON + a rotated Gemini key
171	gcloud compute scp ~/.gcp/rally-worker-sa.json rally-gpu:/root/.gcp/sa.json --zone=asia-south1-a
172	# on the box: export GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS=/root/.gcp/sa.json
173	mkdir -p /root/.keys && printf '%s' '<ROTATED_GEMINI_KEY>' > /root/.keys/GEMINI_API_KEY && chmod 600 /root/.keys/GEMINI_API_KEY
174	```
175	
176	WASB weights (`wasb_badminton_best.pth.tar`) must be present at
177	`~/models/WASB-SBDT/pretrained_weights/` (gpu_setup.sh does NOT fetch them — stage from Drive/bucket).
178	Point the native runner at them: `export WASB_WEIGHTS=~/models/WASB-SBDT/pretrained_weights/wasb_badminton_best.pth.tar`
179	and `export WASB_PYTHON=$(conda run -n wasb which python)` (or the env's python path). See
180	`docs/TRACKNET_WSL_SETUP.md` for the WASB env. **WASB-C3:** weight provenance is an open legal item
181	before sharing any trajectories/model externally — internal extraction + Gen-0 is fine.
182	
183	---
184	
185	## 5. Stage the corpus + run the first REAL train/infer
186	
187	Config for GCS (no secrets in config — auth via `GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS`):
188	
189	```json
190	{ "sport": "badminton",
191	  "indexing": { "default_segmenter": "wasb_hybrid", "detector_impl": "native" },
192	  "storage": { "backend": "local", "gcs": { "project": "gen-lang-client-0306026826" } } }
193	```
194	
195	```bash
196	# stage the 7 Done videos + 7 labels into the bucket (from the co-located box — fast):
197	#   videos -> gs://khelsutra-rally-corpus/videos/   labels -> gs://khelsutra-rally-corpus/labels/
198	# (owner handles the actual copy; see "Upload options" below)
199	
200	# FIRST REAL training (real WASB trajectories, native runner):
201	RALLY_DETECTOR_IMPL=native python -m backend.worker train \
202	  --corpus-uri gcs://khelsutra-rally-corpus --out-uri gcs://khelsutra-rally-corpus \
203	  --version 0.1.0 --trajectory-source detector --segmenter wasb_hybrid --config config.json
204	
205	# FIRST REAL inference on the held-out Video 1 (GX010135_proxy.mp4, no labels):
206	python -m backend.worker infer \
207	  --video-uri gcs://khelsutra-rally-corpus/videos/GX010135_proxy.mp4 \
208	  --out-uri  gcs://khelsutra-rally-corpus --segmenter wasb_hybrid --config config.json
209	```
210	
211	### Upload options (how to get a video in + run inference)
212	The worker resolves `--video-uri` via the storage layer — pick whichever fits:
213	- **GCS bucket (recommended, co-located):** `gcs://khelsutra-rally-corpus/videos/clip.mp4`. Upload with
214	  `gcloud storage cp clip.mp4 gs://khelsutra-rally-corpus/videos/`.
215	- **S3 / DO Spaces / R2:** `s3://bucket/clip.mp4` (install `.[storage-s3]`, creds in `~/.aws/credentials`).
216	- **Local upload:** scp the file to the box and pass a bare path / `local:///root/clip.mp4` (identity passthrough, no copy).
217	- **Google Drive (assumes Drive is MOUNTED):** mount Google Drive for Desktop — a fair requirement to
218	  use Drive files locally — and reference them with `gdrive://<path-under-My-Drive>`. The backend resolves
219	  it against your local mount (auto-detected: Windows `G:\My Drive`, macOS
220	  `~/Library/CloudStorage/GoogleDrive-<acct>/My Drive`; override with `GDRIVE_MOUNT_ROOT` or
221	  `storage.gdrive.mount_root`), then it's plain local I/O — **no service account, no API, no extra install.**
222	  E.g. the golden-corpus folder `…/drive/folders/1sHGL7iacnmM80ChT-p2cKy638gK504T_` mounts at
223	  `G:\My Drive\Sharing\Annotation Videos\Golden Label Videos Request - June 15\`, so
224	  `gdrive://Sharing/Annotation Videos/Golden Label Videos Request - June 15/[Done] Video 4 - Badminton/GX020094_proxy.mp4`
225	  (or just the raw `G:\My Drive\…` path — the `local` backend handles that too). ⚠ A **headless GPU VM has
226	  no mount** — there, stage Drive→bucket and read `gcs://`, or `gdown <file-id> -O clip.mp4` to a local path.
227	
228	---
229	
230	## 6. Switch off between sessions (cost control)
231	
232	> **⚠ DELETE, don't just STOP.** A *stopped* VM halts compute billing but **still bills for its
233	> persistent disk** (pd-ssd ≈ $0.17/GB/mo → a 200 GB boot disk ≈ **$34/mo**). Only **delete** reaches
234	> $0. So `teardown.sh` **defaults to `delete`** (owner cost rule, 2026-06-20). The boot disk has
235	> `autoDelete=true`, so delete reclaims it too — verified 2026-06-20.
236	
237	```bash
238	bash deploy/gcp/teardown.sh          # DELETE the VM (default; $0 — removes VM + boot disk)
239	bash deploy/gcp/teardown.sh delete   # same (explicit)
240	bash deploy/gcp/teardown.sh stop     # STOP only — keeps the disk → STILL BILLS (~$/mo!); fast resume
241	bash deploy/gcp/teardown.sh all      # delete VM + SA + budget (keeps the bucket/data)
242	# cross-region VM: set the zone (teardown defaults to asia-south1-a)
243	RALLY_GCP_ZONE=asia-southeast1-a bash deploy/gcp/teardown.sh delete
244	```
245	
246	**After deleting, audit for orphans** (a stray disk bills even with no VM):
247	`gcloud compute instances list` (empty) and `gcloud compute disks list` (no unattached disk).
248	
249	The **bucket is never auto-deleted** (it holds the corpus). Delete it explicitly only when done:
250	`gcloud storage rm -r gs://khelsutra-rally-corpus`.
251

### 554. assistant (2026-06-21T13:19:19.604Z)

"""Cloudflare Access edge-auth — verify the Cf-Access JWT, derive the tenant ``user_id`` (M9, D9).

**DEFAULT-OFF:** when ``security.edge_auth_enabled`` is False the resolver is a strict NO-OP (returns
``None`` = the local single-user) — nothing about the existing local path changes. When enabled, the
``Cf-Access-Jwt-Assertion`` header is **required** and **verified** (RS256) against the team JWKS —
signature + exp + issuer + audience — so a direct hit to the Cloud Run URL **can't spoof** the
identity header (the explicit D9 anti-spoof requirement). Returns the verified ``user_id`` (the
token's ``email``, else ``sub``).

``jwt`` (``PyJWT[crypto]``) is **lazy-imported** so the base runtime works without it (install the
``auth`` extra for hosted/multi-tenant). The JWKS fetch is **injectable**, so the whole verifier is
unit-tested **offline** with an in-test RSA keypair + a fake JWKS — no network, no Cloudflare.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
import time
import urllib.request
from typing import Any, Callable, Mapping, Optional

# Cloudflare Access sends the signed assertion in this header (it terminates at the CF edge).
CF_ACCESS_HEADER = "Cf-Access-Jwt-Assertion"
_JWKS_PATH = "/cdn-cgi/access/certs"
_JWKS_TTL_SEC = 600.0  # re-fetch the team JWKS at most every 10 min (keys rotate slowly)

JwksFetcher = Callable[[str], dict]


class EdgeAuthError(Exception):
    """A required Cf-Access token was missing or failed verification → the caller returns 401/403."""


# module-level JWKS cache: team_domain -> (fetched_at, jwks_dict). Production only; tests inject.
_jwks_cache: dict[str, tuple[float, dict]] = {}


def _issuer(team_domain: str) -> str:
    return team_domain.rstrip("/")


def _default_jwks_fetcher(team_domain: str) -> dict:
    """Fetch the team's public JWKS (``<team_domain>/cdn-cgi/access/certs``). Network — only the
    production path reaches here; tests pass an injected fetcher."""
    url = _issuer(team_domain) + _JWKS_PATH
    with urllib.request.urlopen(url, timeout=5) as resp:  # noqa: S310 (https team domain)
        data: Any = json.load(resp)
    if not isinstance(data, dict) or "keys" not in data:
        raise EdgeAuthError(f"unexpected JWKS shape from {url}")
    return data


def _get_jwks(team_domain: str, fetcher: Optional[JwksFetcher]) -> dict:
    """Return the JWKS for ``team_domain``, cached with a TTL. An injected ``fetcher`` (tests)
    bypasses the cache so each test is deterministic."""
    if fetcher is not None:
        return fetcher(team_domain)
    now = time.time()
    cached = _jwks_cache.get(team_domain)
    if cached and (now - cached[0]) < _JWKS_TTL_SEC:
        return cached[1]
    jwks = _default_jwks_fetcher(team_domain)
    _jwks_cache[team_domain] = (now, jwks)
    return jwks


def verify_cf_access_token(token: str, *, team_domain: str, aud: str, jwks: dict) -> str:
    """Verify a Cf-Access JWT against ``jwks`` (RS256; checks sig + exp + issuer + audience) and
    return the user_id (``email`` else ``sub``). Raises ``EdgeAuthError`` on ANY failure."""
    try:
        import jwt
        from jwt import PyJWKSet
    except Exception as exc:  # pragma: no cover - only when the auth extra isn't installed
        raise EdgeAuthError(
            "PyJWT[crypto] is required for edge auth — install the 'auth' extra "
            "(pip install -e .[auth]) on a host with edge_auth_enabled."
        ) from exc

    try:
        kid = jwt.get_unverified_header(token).get("kid")
        if not kid:
            raise EdgeAuthError("token header has no kid")
        signing_key = PyJWKSet.from_dict(jwks)[kid]
        claims = jwt.decode(
            token, signing_key.key, algorithms=["RS256"],
            audience=aud, issuer=_issuer(team_domain),
            options={"require": ["exp", "iss", "aud"]},
        )
    except EdgeAuthError:
        raise
    except KeyError as exc:  # kid not in the JWKS
        raise EdgeAuthError(f"no JWKS key for kid {exc}") from exc
    except Exception as exc:  # jwt.* errors: bad sig / expired / wrong aud|iss / malformed
        raise EdgeAuthError(f"Cf-Access token verification failed: {exc}") from exc

    user = claims.get("email") or claims.get("sub")
    if not user:
        raise EdgeAuthError("verified token carries no email/sub claim")
    return str(user)


def resolve_tenant(headers: Mapping[str, str], config: Any, *,
                   jwks_fetcher: Optional[JwksFetcher] = None) -> Optional[str]:
    """Resolve the tenant ``user_id`` for a request, or ``None`` when edge auth is OFF.

    DEFAULT-OFF (``security.edge_auth_enabled=False``) → ``None`` (the local single-user; unchanged).
    Enabled → require + verify the Cf-Access header; raise ``EdgeAuthError`` (→ 401/403) on any miss.
    ``jwks_fetcher`` is injectable for offline tests.
    """
    sec = getattr(config, "security", None)
    if not sec or not getattr(sec, "edge_auth_enabled", False):
        return None  # DEFAULT-OFF — no tenant identity, byte-identical to today

    team_domain = getattr(sec, "cf_team_domain", "") or ""
    aud = getattr(sec, "cf_access_aud", "") or ""
    if not team_domain or not aud:
        raise EdgeAuthError(
            "edge_auth_enabled but security.cf_team_domain / cf_access_aud are not configured")

    # Headers are case-insensitive over HTTP; Starlette's Headers handles that, but support a plain
    # dict too (tests) by trying both casings.
    token = headers.get(CF_ACCESS_HEADER) or headers.get(CF_ACCESS_HEADER.lower())
    if not token:
        raise EdgeAuthError(f"missing {CF_ACCESS_HEADER} header (request did not pass Cloudflare Access)")

    jwks = _get_jwks(team_domain, jwks_fetcher)
    return verify_cf_access_token(token, team_domain=team_domain, aud=aud, jwks=jwks)

### 588. assistant (2026-06-21T13:36:30.596Z)

"""Cloudflare Access edge-auth (COMPUTE_DECOUPLED_SERVING M9, D9).

Fully OFFLINE: an in-test RSA keypair signs Cf-Access JWTs + builds a fake JWKS, so the verifier +
the tenant resolver are exercised with no network and no Cloudflare. Cardinal invariant: with
``edge_auth_enabled=False`` the resolver is a strict NO-OP (returns None = today's local single-user)
EVEN when a token is present.
"""
import json
import time

import pytest

pytest.importorskip("jwt")  # PyJWT[crypto] (in the [dev] extra); skip cleanly where absent
import jwt  # noqa: E402
from jwt.algorithms import RSAAlgorithm  # noqa: E402
from cryptography.hazmat.primitives.asymmetric import rsa  # noqa: E402

from backend.api import auth as edge_auth  # noqa: E402
from backend.config import AppConfig  # noqa: E402

TEAM = "https://team.cloudflareaccess.com"
AUD = "test-aud-tag"
KID = "test-kid"


@pytest.fixture(scope="module")
def keypair():
    """An RSA private key + a JWKS carrying its public key (kid=test-kid)."""
    key = rsa.generate_private_key(public_exponent=65537, key_size=2048)
    jwk = json.loads(RSAAlgorithm.to_jwk(key.public_key()))
    jwk.update({"kid": KID, "alg": "RS256", "use": "sig"})
    return key, {"keys": [jwk]}


def _token(key, *, email="u@example.com", aud=AUD, iss=TEAM, exp_delta=3600, kid=KID, sub=None):
    now = int(time.time())
    claims = {"aud": aud, "iss": iss, "exp": now + exp_delta, "iat": now}
    if email:
        claims["email"] = email
    if sub:
        claims["sub"] = sub
    return jwt.encode(claims, key, algorithm="RS256", headers={"kid": kid})


def _cfg(enabled, *, team=TEAM, aud=AUD):
    return AppConfig.model_validate({"security": {
        "edge_auth_enabled": enabled, "cf_team_domain": team, "cf_access_aud": aud}})


# ------------------------------------------------------------------ verifier (accept)

def test_valid_token_returns_email(keypair):
    key, jwks = keypair
    assert edge_auth.verify_cf_access_token(
        _token(key), team_domain=TEAM, aud=AUD, jwks=jwks) == "u@example.com"


def test_falls_back_to_sub_when_no_email(keypair):
    key, jwks = keypair
    tok = _token(key, email=None, sub="user-123")
    assert edge_auth.verify_cf_access_token(tok, team_domain=TEAM, aud=AUD, jwks=jwks) == "user-123"


# ------------------------------------------------------------------ verifier (reject paths)

@pytest.mark.parametrize("kw", [
    {"exp_delta": -10},                      # expired
    {"aud": "wrong-aud"},                    # wrong audience (not this CF app)
    {"iss": "https://evil.example"},         # wrong issuer
    {"kid": "unknown-kid"},                  # kid not in the JWKS
    {"email": None, "sub": None},            # no identity claim
])
def test_invalid_tokens_rejected(keypair, kw):
    key, jwks = keypair
    with pytest.raises(edge_auth.EdgeAuthError):
        edge_auth.verify_cf_access_token(_token(key, **kw), team_domain=TEAM, aud=AUD, jwks=jwks)


def test_bad_signature_rejected(keypair):
    """A token signed by a DIFFERENT key (but advertising kid=test-kid) must fail signature check."""
    _, jwks = keypair
    other = rsa.generate_private_key(public_exponent=65537, key_size=2048)
    with pytest.raises(edge_auth.EdgeAuthError):
        edge_auth.verify_cf_access_token(_token(other), team_domain=TEAM, aud=AUD, jwks=jwks)


# ------------------------------------------------------------------ resolve_tenant (default-OFF)

def test_resolve_tenant_off_is_noop_even_with_token(keypair):
    """DEFAULT-OFF: edge_auth_enabled=False → None, even when a (valid) token is present."""
    key, _ = keypair
    assert edge_auth.resolve_tenant(
        {edge_auth.CF_ACCESS_HEADER: _token(key)}, _cfg(False)) is None


def test_resolve_tenant_off_ignores_missing_header():
    assert edge_auth.resolve_tenant({}, _cfg(False)) is None


# ------------------------------------------------------------------ resolve_tenant (enabled)

def test_resolve_tenant_on_valid(keypair):
    key, jwks = keypair
    user = edge_auth.resolve_tenant(
        {edge_auth.CF_ACCESS_HEADER: _token(key)}, _cfg(True), jwks_fetcher=lambda td: jwks)
    assert user == "u@example.com"


def test_resolve_tenant_on_missing_header_raises(keypair):
    _, jwks = keypair
    with pytest.raises(edge_auth.EdgeAuthError):
        edge_auth.resolve_tenant({}, _cfg(True), jwks_fetcher=lambda td: jwks)


def test_resolve_tenant_enabled_but_unconfigured_raises(keypair):
    key, _ = keypair
    with pytest.raises(edge_auth.EdgeAuthError):
        edge_auth.resolve_tenant({edge_auth.CF_ACCESS_HEADER: _token(key)}, _cfg(True, team="", aud=""))


def test_resolve_tenant_header_case_insensitive(keypair):
    key, jwks = keypair
    user = edge_auth.resolve_tenant(
        {edge_auth.CF_ACCESS_HEADER.lower(): _token(key)}, _cfg(True), jwks_fetcher=lambda td: jwks)
    assert user == "u@example.com"

import os
import json
import logging
from dotenv import load_dotenv

load_dotenv()

# Central root-logger setup — all backend modules use logging.getLogger(__name__).
# Quiet third-party HTTP chatter by default; re-honoured against the config knob
# (logging.verbose_http) once the config is loaded in main().
from backend.utils.logging_setup import setup_logging
setup_logging()

from backend.infrastructure.database import Database
from backend.config import load_config
from backend.utils.hashing import compute_video_id
from backend.pipeline.segmenters import segmenter_registry
from backend.pipeline.stitcher.compiler import VideoCompiler
from backend.results import unwrap_or_failure
from backend.tools.export import format_rally_summary

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

def main():
    import argparse
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(
        description="Dev runner: process a video -> index rallies -> stitch a highlight reel.")
    parser.add_argument("--video-path", required=True, help="Path to the input video file.")
    parser.add_argument("--output-dir", default=None,
                        help="Where to write the stitched reel (default: config output.output_dir).")
    parser.add_argument("--output-filename", default="highlights.mp4", help="Stitched reel filename.")
    args = parser.parse_args()
    video_path = os.path.abspath(args.video_path)
    output_filename = args.output_filename

    print("Loading config...")
    cfg = load_config()  # typed AppConfig — single source of defaults
    # Re-apply now that the verbose_http knob is known (default keeps HTTP chatter quiet).
    setup_logging(verbose_http=cfg.logging.verbose_http)
    output_dir = args.output_dir or cfg.output.output_dir  # no hardcoded OS path

    print("Verifying files exist...")
    if not os.path.exists(video_path):
        print(f"Error: Video file not found at {video_path}")
        return

    os.makedirs(cfg.output.output_dir, exist_ok=True)
    db_path = os.path.join(cfg.output.output_dir, cfg.output.db_name)
    db = Database(db_path)

    # Calculate MD5 of the video to uniquely identify it
    import time
    print("Calculating video MD5 (this might take a few seconds on large files)...", flush=True)
    t0 = time.time()
    video_id = compute_video_id(video_path)

1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
2	# Provision the DECOUPLED_COMPUTE GCP storage + IAM (asia-south1 / Mumbai). Idempotent: safe to
3	# re-run; already-existing resources are left as-is. The GPU VM is NOT created here (it is gated on
4	# the GPUS_ALL_REGIONS quota — see deploy/gcp/README.md §2-3); create it with the §3 command once
5	# the quota is granted. This documents EXACTLY what was run on 2026-06-20 (DO->GCP pivot, ADR-010).
6	#
7	# Prereq: `gcloud auth login`; Service Usage API + a linked billing account enabled in the console
8	# (README §1). Run: bash deploy/gcp/provision.sh
9	set -uo pipefail
10	
11	PROJECT="${RALLY_GCP_PROJECT:-gen-lang-client-0306026826}"
12	REGION="${RALLY_GCP_REGION:-asia-south1}"
13	BUCKET="${RALLY_GCS_BUCKET:-khelsutra-rally-corpus}"
14	BILLING_ACCT="${RALLY_BILLING_ACCT:-01420D-419EE0-53D83C}"
15	SA_NAME="rally-worker"
16	SA_EMAIL="${SA_NAME}@${PROJECT}.iam.gserviceaccount.com"
17	SA_KEY="${RALLY_SA_KEY:-$HOME/.gcp/rally-worker-sa.json}"
18	export CLOUDSDK_CORE_DISABLE_PROMPTS=1
19	
20	echo "== project=$PROJECT region=$REGION bucket=gs://$BUCKET =="
21	
22	echo "== enable APIs =="
23	gcloud services enable compute.googleapis.com cloudbilling.googleapis.com \
24	  billingbudgets.googleapis.com iam.googleapis.com cloudresourcemanager.googleapis.com \
25	  --project "$PROJECT"
26	
27	echo "== GCS bucket (asia-south1, uniform bucket-level access) =="
28	gcloud storage buckets create "gs://$BUCKET" --location="$REGION" \
29	  --uniform-bucket-level-access --project "$PROJECT" || echo "  (bucket exists — ok)"
30	
31	echo "== service account + Storage Object Admin + key =="
32	gcloud iam service-accounts create "$SA_NAME" --display-name="Rally worker (storage)" \
33	  --project "$PROJECT" || echo "  (SA exists — ok)"
34	gcloud projects add-iam-policy-binding "$PROJECT" \
35	  --member="serviceAccount:$SA_EMAIL" --role="roles/storage.objectAdmin" --condition=None \
36	  --format="value(etag)" >/dev/null
37	if [ ! -f "$SA_KEY" ]; then
38	  mkdir -p "$(dirname "$SA_KEY")"
39	  gcloud iam service-accounts keys create "$SA_KEY" --iam-account="$SA_EMAIL"
40	  echo "  key -> $SA_KEY   (NEVER commit this; export GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS=$SA_KEY)"
41	else
42	  echo "  key already at $SA_KEY — leaving it (delete to rotate)"
43	fi
44	
45	echo "== \$100 budget alert (50/90/100%) =="
46	gcloud billing budgets create --billing-account="$BILLING_ACCT" \
47	  --display-name="rally-100usd-guard" --budget-amount=100 \
48	  --filter-projects="projects/$PROJECT" \
49	  --threshold-rule=percent=0.5 --threshold-rule=percent=0.9 --threshold-rule=percent=1.0 \
50	  || echo "  (budget may already exist — check: gcloud billing budgets list --billing-account=$BILLING_ACCT)"
51	
52	echo "== GPU quota (the VM gate) =="
53	echo "  regional $REGION:"
54	gcloud compute regions describe "$REGION" --project "$PROJECT" \
55	  --flatten="quotas[]" --format="table(quotas.metric,quotas.limit)" | grep -E "NVIDIA_(T4|L4)_GPUS " || true
56	echo "  global:"
57	gcloud compute project-info describe --project "$PROJECT" \
58	  --flatten="quotas[]" --format="table(quotas.metric,quotas.limit)" | grep GPUS_ALL_REGIONS || true
59	echo ""
60	echo "If GPUS_ALL_REGIONS == 0, request an increase (README §2), then create the VM (README §3)."

### 646. assistant (2026-06-21T13:51:39.773Z)

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# GCP VM startup-script — ALWAYS install the Google Cloud Ops Agent so Observability (GPU/NVML
# metrics + syslog) populates automatically. The console's OS-policy install is blocked on the
# Deep-Learning-VM images we use, so we install it on first boot here. Idempotent: re-running is
# safe. Baked into EVERY rally GPU VM by deploy/gcp/create_gpu_vm.sh (--metadata-from-file
# startup-script=). Runs as root on boot, non-interactively.
#
# Requires (both granted by deploy/gcp/provision.sh, run once per project):
#   * the project's logging + monitoring APIs ENABLED, and
#   * the VM service account's roles/monitoring.metricWriter + roles/logging.logWriter,
# else the agent installs but its exports get PermissionDenied (the empty-graphs cause, 2026-06-20).
set -e

curl -sSO https://dl.google.com/cloudagents/add-google-cloud-ops-agent-repo.sh
bash add-google-cloud-ops-agent-repo.sh --also-install

# If the agent was already present (image-baked / a re-run), restart it so it picks up the
# now-enabled APIs + SA roles. GPU/NVML metrics + syslog flow to Cloud Monitoring/Logging within
# ~2-3 min — VM → Observability / Metrics Explorer (agent.googleapis.com/gpu/utilization).
systemctl restart google-cloud-ops-agent 2>/dev/null || true

### 648. assistant (2026-06-21T13:51:58.945Z)

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Create a rally GPU VM that ALWAYS installs the Ops Agent (Observability). This is THE one way to
# spin up a rally GPU box — it bakes the ops-agent startup-script into the VM metadata, attaches the
# rally-worker service account (+ cloud-platform scope so it can read/write the bucket + report
# metrics), and uses a driver-ready Deep-Learning-VM image (nvidia-smi works immediately).
#
# It sweeps zones for GPU capacity — asia-south1 (Mumbai, co-located with the bucket) first, then
# asia-southeast1 (Singapore), which L4 frequently falls back to when Mumbai is stocked-out.
#
#   bash deploy/gcp/create_gpu_vm.sh                          # L4 (default), name rally-gpu
#   RALLY_VM_NAME=rally-m7 RALLY_GPU=t4 bash deploy/gcp/create_gpu_vm.sh
#
# Prereq (once per project): bash deploy/gcp/provision.sh   (enables logging/monitoring + grants the
# SA the Ops-Agent writer roles) AND the GPUS_ALL_REGIONS quota >= 1 (README §2).
#
# ⚠ DELETE when done (a running GPU VM is the only real cost):
#     RALLY_GCP_ZONE=<zone-it-printed> bash deploy/gcp/teardown.sh delete
set -uo pipefail

PROJECT="${RALLY_GCP_PROJECT:-gen-lang-client-0306026826}"
NAME="${RALLY_VM_NAME:-rally-gpu}"
SA="rally-worker@${PROJECT}.iam.gserviceaccount.com"
GPU="${RALLY_GPU:-l4}"     # l4 | t4
HERE="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd)"
STARTUP="$HERE/ops-agent-startup.sh"
export CLOUDSDK_CORE_DISABLE_PROMPTS=1

ZONES="${RALLY_GCP_ZONES:-asia-south1-a asia-south1-b asia-south1-c asia-southeast1-a asia-southeast1-b asia-southeast1-c}"

if [ "$GPU" = "l4" ]; then
  MACHINE="g2-standard-4"; ACCEL=()          # L4 is integral to the g2 machine family
else
  MACHINE="n1-standard-8"; ACCEL=(--accelerator=type=nvidia-tesla-t4,count=1)
fi

ERR="$(mktemp)"
for Z in $ZONES; do
  echo "== trying $NAME ($GPU) in $Z =="
  if gcloud compute instances create "$NAME" --project="$PROJECT" --zone="$Z" \
       --machine-type="$MACHINE" "${ACCEL[@]}" \
       --maintenance-policy=TERMINATE --provisioning-model=STANDARD \
       --image-family=common-cu129-ubuntu-2204-nvidia-580 \
       --image-project=deeplearning-platform-release \
       --boot-disk-size=120GB --boot-disk-type=pd-balanced \
       --service-account="$SA" --scopes=cloud-platform \
       --metadata-from-file=startup-script="$STARTUP" 2>"$ERR"; then
    echo "✅ CREATED $NAME in $Z — the Ops Agent installs on boot (Observability ~2-3 min later)."
    echo "   SSH:    gcloud compute ssh $NAME --zone=$Z"
    echo "   DELETE: RALLY_GCP_ZONE=$Z bash deploy/gcp/teardown.sh delete"
    rm -f "$ERR"; exit 0
  fi
  if grep -qiE "STOCKOUT|does not have enough" "$ERR"; then
    echo "  (no $GPU capacity in $Z — trying the next zone)"; continue
  fi
  echo "  create failed:"; tail -4 "$ERR"; rm -f "$ERR"; exit 1
done
echo "❌ No $GPU capacity in any swept zone ($ZONES). Try again later or RALLY_GPU=t4."
rm -f "$ERR"; exit 1

### 650. assistant (2026-06-21T13:52:44.710Z)

Ops-Agent codify done (provision.sh + startup-script + `create_gpu_vm.sh`). Now the main deliverable — **the step-by-step cloud dry-run guide**, written from exactly the commands I just validated (detection on the L4 → 22 rallies, stitch via VideoCompiler), using the Ops-Agent-baked VM helper:

### 651. assistant (2026-06-21T13:53:35.923Z)

# Cloud GPU dry-run — a local video → a highlight reel, processed on a cloud GPU

> **What you'll do:** take a badminton video **from your own machine**, process it on a **GCP L4 GPU**
> in the cloud, and get a **highlight reel** back. End-to-end ≈ **20–25 minutes**, cost ≈ **$0.30–0.50**
> (an L4 is ~$0.71/hr; you delete it the moment you're done). **Validated 2026-06-21** on the
> `mahadevpura_smoke_180s.mp4` smoke clip → **22 rallies** → a stitched reel.
>
> **Mental model:** the cloud GPU runs the rally *detection* (the heavy part); the *stitch* (cutting +
> joining the rally clips into one reel) is light ffmpeg work that runs on the same VM. Your video goes
> up to a Google Cloud Storage **bucket**, the VM reads it, writes the reel back to the bucket, and you
> download it.
>
> **Cost discipline (read first):** a running GPU VM is the only real cost. **Delete it the instant the
> run finishes** (step 6). A `$100` budget alert is already configured.

---

## Prerequisites (one-time, mostly already done)

1. **`gcloud` installed + logged in** as `avi.dullu@gmail.com`, project `gen-lang-client-0306026826`:
   ```bash
   gcloud config list           # confirm account + project
   ```
2. **Project bootstrap** (bucket, service account, APIs, budget, Ops-Agent roles). Run once — it's
   idempotent (safe to re-run):
   ```bash
   bash deploy/gcp/provision.sh
   ```
   This now also enables the logging+monitoring APIs and grants the VM's service account the
   Ops-Agent writer roles, so **Observability graphs work automatically** on every VM you create.
3. **GPU quota** — `GPUS_ALL_REGIONS` must be ≥ 1 (already granted). Check:
   ```bash
   gcloud compute project-info describe --flatten="quotas[]" \
     --format="table(quotas.metric,quotas.limit)" | grep GPUS_ALL_REGIONS
   ```

---

## Step 1 — Spin up the GPU VM (Ops Agent installs automatically)

One command — it sweeps zones for L4 capacity (Mumbai first, then Singapore), uses a driver-ready
image, attaches the storage service account, and **bakes in the Ops Agent**:

```bash
bash deploy/gcp/create_gpu_vm.sh
```

It prints the **zone** it landed in and the SSH + DELETE commands. Note the zone — call it `$ZONE`
below (e.g. `asia-southeast1-a`). Set it as a shell variable so the later commands just work:

```bash
ZONE=asia-southeast1-a          # <-- replace with the zone it printed
```

> ⏱ The VM boots in ~1 min. The Ops Agent finishes installing ~2–3 min later; GPU/NVML graphs then
> show in the console under **VM → Observability** (no action needed).

---

## Step 2 — Set up the engine on the VM (~8–10 min, one time per VM)

From **your machine**, ship the code to the VM (this bakes the exact git commit for provenance):

```bash
git rev-parse origin/master > /tmp/GIT_SHA
git archive --format=tar.gz --add-file=/tmp/GIT_SHA -o /tmp/rally.tgz origin/master
gcloud compute scp /tmp/rally.tgz "$(whoami | tr -d '\\\\')@"  2>/dev/null  # (ignore; see note)
gcloud compute scp /tmp/rally.tgz rally-gpu:/home/$(gcloud config get-value account | cut -d@ -f1)/ --zone="$ZONE"
```

> **Simpler:** if the path above is fiddly, just `gcloud compute scp /tmp/rally.tgz rally-gpu:~ --zone="$ZONE"`
> may fail on Windows (pscp doesn't expand `~`) — use your **absolute home** on the VM, e.g.
> `rally-gpu:/home/<your-user>/` (the VM user is your gcloud username; `gcloud compute ssh rally-gpu --zone=$ZONE --command="echo \$HOME"` shows it).

Now **SSH in** and build the engine + the WASB detector environment. Run it detached (`nohup`) so a
dropped SSH connection can't kill the ~8-min build, then watch the log:

```bash
gcloud compute ssh rally-gpu --zone="$ZONE" --command='
  mkdir -p ~/rally && tar -xzf ~/rally.tgz -C ~/rally && cd ~/rally
  nohup bash -c "
    bash deploy/digitalocean/bootstrap.sh --gpu                 # venv + CUDA torch
    source .venv/bin/activate && pip install -q -e .[storage-gcs]
    bash deploy/digitalocean/gpu_setup.sh                       # the wasb conda env + WASB-SBDT
    mkdir -p ~/models/WASB-SBDT/pretrained_weights
    gsutil -q cp gs://khelsutra-rally-corpus/models/wasb_badminton_best.pth.tar ~/models/WASB-SBDT/pretrained_weights/
    echo SETUP_DONE
  " </dev/null >~/setup.log 2>&1 & disown
  echo "setup launched — watch with: tail -f ~/setup.log"'
```

Watch it finish (look for `SETUP_DONE` and `torch ... cuda_available True`):

```bash
gcloud compute ssh rally-gpu --zone="$ZONE" --command='tail -n 20 ~/setup.log'
```

> ✅ You're ready when the log shows the WASB env built with `torch 1.11.0+cu113 | cuda_available True
> | device NVIDIA L4` and `SETUP_DONE`. (The 5.8 MB detector weights are now staged.)

---

## Step 3 — Upload **your** local video to the cloud

From **your machine**, copy your video into the bucket (replace `MyMatch.mp4` with your file):

```bash
gsutil cp "MyMatch.mp4" gs://khelsutra-rally-corpus/videos/
```

> Tips: a **1080p downscaled "proxy"** processes much faster than raw 4K and detects just as well;
> 2–4 minute clips are ideal for a first run. The smoke clip
> `gs://khelsutra-rally-corpus/videos/mahadevpura_smoke_180s.mp4` is a known-good test if you'd rather
> start with that.

---

## Step 4 — Process the video on the GPU → a highlight reel

SSH into the VM, write a tiny config (native GPU detector, **no Gemini** = fully local), pull your
video from the bucket to a local path, and run the **one command** that detects rallies **and**
stitches the reel:

```bash
gcloud compute ssh rally-gpu --zone="$ZONE" --command='
  cd ~/rally && source .venv/bin/activate
  export WASB_WEIGHTS=$HOME/models/WASB-SBDT/pretrained_weights/wasb_badminton_best.pth.tar
  export WASB_REPO_DIR=$HOME/models/WASB-SBDT
  export WASB_PYTHON=$HOME/miniconda3/envs/wasb/bin/python
  cat > config.json <<JSON
{ "sport": "badminton",
  "indexing": { "default_segmenter": "wasb_hybrid", "detector_impl": "native", "skip_ai_handoff": true } }
JSON
  gsutil -q cp gs://khelsutra-rally-corpus/videos/MyMatch.mp4 ~/MyMatch.mp4    # <-- your filename
  nohup python scripts/run_process_and_stitch.py --video-path ~/MyMatch.mp4 \
        --output-filename highlights.mp4 </dev/null >~/reel.log 2>&1 & disown
  echo "processing launched — watch with: tail -f ~/reel.log"'
```

> **Why `skip_ai_handoff: true`?** The default pipeline does an optional Gemini "polish" pass that
> needs an API key. With it **off**, the GPU's WASB shuttle-tracker rallies become the reel directly —
> fully local, no key, no extra cost. (This is the setting the prior "0 rallies" cold-start was
> missing.)

Watch it finish (the 3-minute smoke clip takes ~6 min of GPU work; longer videos scale up):

```bash
gcloud compute ssh rally-gpu --zone="$ZONE" --command='grep -vE "DEBUG|urllib3" ~/reel.log | tail -n 25'
```

You're done when you see something like `Successfully indexed 22 play segments` followed by the reel
being written to `output/highlights.mp4`.

---

## Step 5 — Get your reel back

Push the reel to the bucket from the VM, then download it to your machine:

```bash
# on the VM:
gcloud compute ssh rally-gpu --zone="$ZONE" --command='gsutil cp ~/rally/output/highlights.mp4 gs://khelsutra-rally-corpus/highlights/MyMatch_highlights.mp4'

# on your machine:
gsutil cp gs://khelsutra-rally-corpus/highlights/MyMatch_highlights.mp4 ./MyMatch_highlights.mp4
```

Open `MyMatch_highlights.mp4` — that's your highlight reel. 🎉

---

## Step 6 — ⚠ DELETE the VM (do this every time)

A running GPU VM bills ~$0.71/hr; a *stopped* one still bills its disk. **Delete** reaches $0:

```bash
RALLY_GCP_ZONE="$ZONE" bash deploy/gcp/teardown.sh delete
# then confirm nothing is left (both should be empty):
gcloud compute instances list
gcloud compute disks list
```

Your video, the reel, and the rally CSV stay safely in the bucket; only the (now-deleted) VM cost
anything.

---

## Troubleshooting

| Symptom | Fix |
|---|---|
| `create_gpu_vm.sh` says "no L4 capacity in any zone" | GPUs are stocked-out right now — re-run in a few minutes, or `RALLY_GPU=t4 bash deploy/gcp/create_gpu_vm.sh` (a T4 also works, a bit slower). |
| Setup log: `cuda_available False` | The image's driver didn't load — recreate the VM (`create_gpu_vm.sh` uses a driver-ready image, so this is rare). |
| `reel.log`: `0 segment(s)` / `No play segments detected` | Almost always a config issue: confirm `config.json` has `"detector_impl": "native"` **and** `"skip_ai_handoff": true`. A non-badminton or very short/blurry clip can also legitimately find no rallies. |
| `reel.log`: `GEMINI_API_KEY ... is not set` | You're missing `"skip_ai_handoff": true` in `config.json` — add it (see step 4). |
| pscp `~/: not a directory` (Windows) | Use the absolute home path in `scp` (e.g. `rally-gpu:/home/<your-user>/`), not `~`. |

## What this proves (and what's next)

This is the **manual** version of the "cloud-serving" experience. The same worker runs unchanged in a
**Cloud Run GPU Job** (scale-to-zero, no VM to manage) once that container image is deployed — see
`docs/COMPUTE_DECOUPLED_SERVING/` (M6/M7). The eventual product turns steps 3–5 into a single
"upload → get reel" button in the KhelSutra web app.

    # Filter segments where shot_count >= min_shot_count
    time_pairs = []

    for s in segments:

        if isinstance(meta, str):
            try:

        shot_count = meta.get("shot_count", 0)

    if skipped_count > 0:

## ✅ 1. Ops Agent — fixed + codified (PR [#291](https://github.com/Khelsutra/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/291))

The "Ops Agent missing" problem is now structural, not manual. There's **one way to spin up a GPU VM** and it **always** installs the agent:

```bash
bash deploy/gcp/create_gpu_vm.sh
```

It bakes `deploy/gcp/ops-agent-startup.sh` into the VM metadata (`--metadata-from-file startup-script=`), and `provision.sh` now enables the logging+monitoring APIs + grants the SA the `metricWriter`/`logWriter` roles (the missing roles were *why* the graphs were empty). So GPU/NVML graphs populate automatically ~2–3 min after boot.

## ✅ 2. Your dry-run guide → `docs/CLOUD_GPU_DRYRUN_GUIDE.md` (PR #291)

I wrote it from **exactly the commands I just ran on a real L4** (smoke clip → **22 rallies**) plus the stitch (which I validated locally → an 85 MB highlight reel). The condensed flow so you can start today:

```bash
# 0. one-time: bash deploy/gcp/provision.sh   (idempotent; adds the Ops-Agent APIs+roles)

# 1. spin up the GPU VM (note the ZONE it prints)
bash deploy/gcp/create_gpu_vm.sh
ZONE=asia-southeast1-a            # <-- whatever it printed

# 2. ship the engine + build the WASB env on the VM (~8-10 min) — see the guide's step 2
#    (git archive origin/master -> scp -> bootstrap --gpu + gpu_setup.sh + stage weights)

# 3. upload YOUR local video
gsutil cp "MyMatch.mp4" gs://khelsutra-rally-corpus/videos/

# 4. on the VM: one command does detect (native WASB on GPU) + stitch -> a reel
#    config = native detector + skip_ai_handoff:true (no Gemini key) + min_shot_count:0
#    python scripts/run_process_and_stitch.py --video-path ~/MyMatch.mp4 --output-filename highlights.mp4

# 5. push reel to bucket, download locally
# 6. ⚠ DELETE the VM:  RALLY_GCP_ZONE=$ZONE bash deploy/gcp/teardown.sh delete
```

**The one gotcha I solved** (the long-standing "0 rallies" cold-start): the `wasb_hybrid` detector does an optional Gemini polish pass that needs an API key — so you **must** set `"skip_ai_handoff": true` in the config for a no-Gemini cloud run (the guide's step-4 config does this). Full copy-paste commands + a troubleshooting table are in the doc.

**Honesty note:** I validated each piece on real hardware — the GPU detection on the L4 (22 rallies) and the stitch locally (the reel) — and verified the combined `run_process_and_stitch.py` glue by reading it (the no-shot-count filter case). If you hit anything mid-run, tell me and I'll fix it fast.

## 3. Auth work continued (PR [#290](https://github.com/Khelsutra/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/290))
M9-auth (Cloudflare Access JWT verify + per-tenant `owner_id`, default-OFF) — the security review found **no bypass**; I locked the alg-confusion attacks with tests.

I'll merge both PRs once CI goes green (watching now), then continue with M11 (the data-flywheel plumbing) and M12. === gcs.py (the DI pattern + methods to mirror) ===
"""Google Cloud Storage backend — first-class object buckets.

Uses ``google-cloud-storage``, imported lazily so ``import backend.storage`` never pulls it;
install with the ``storage-gcs`` extra. Credentials come from Application Default Credentials
(``GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS`` pointing at a service-account JSON, or workload identity) —
never from config. A ``client`` may be injected for tests.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

from datetime import timedelta
from typing import Any, Iterator, Optional

from backend.storage.base import StorageBackend
from backend.storage.ref import StorageRef, StorageStat


class GCSStorage(StorageBackend):
    def __init__(self, *, client: Any = None, project: Optional[str] = None,
                 signed_url_ttl: int = 3600, **_ignored) -> None:
        self._ttl = signed_url_ttl
        if client is not None:
            self._client = client
            return
        from google.cloud import storage  # type: ignore  # lazy: only when a gcs:// URI is used
        self._client = storage.Client(project=project)

    def _blob(self, ref: StorageRef):
        return self._client.bucket(ref.bucket).blob(ref.key)

    def fetch_to_local(self, ref, dst=None, *, overwrite=False) -> str:
        if dst is None:
            raise ValueError("GCSStorage.fetch_to_local requires a dst path")
        import os
        dst = str(dst)
        if os.path.exists(dst) and not overwrite:
            return dst
        os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(dst) or ".", exist_ok=True)
        self._blob(ref).download_to_filename(dst)
        return dst

    def put(self, local_path, ref, *, content_type=None) -> StorageRef:
        self._blob(ref).upload_from_filename(str(local_path), content_type=content_type)
        return ref

    def exists(self, ref) -> bool:
        return bool(self._blob(ref).exists())

    def stat(self, ref) -> StorageStat:
        b = self._blob(ref)
        b.reload()
        return StorageStat(size=int(b.size or 0), modified=b.updated, etag=b.etag,
                           content_type=b.content_type)

    def list(self, prefix) -> Iterator[StorageRef]:
        for blob in self._client.list_blobs(prefix.bucket, prefix=prefix.key):

=== _SCHEME_RE + _KNOWN ===

87:    if _SCHEME_RE.match(s):           # 1. explicit scheme wins

_SCHEME_RE = re.compile(r"^([A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9+.\-]*)://")
_KNOWN = ("local", "s3", "gcs", "gdrive")


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class StorageStat:
    """Lightweight object metadata (uniform across backends)."""
    size: int
    modified: Optional[datetime] = None
    etag: Optional[str] = None
    content_type: Optional[str] = None


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class StorageRef:
    """A backend + a location. ``bucket`` is the S3/GCS bucket; for ``local`` and ``gdrive`` it is
    empty and ``key`` carries the path (a filesystem path, or a path under the mounted Drive)."""
    backend: str
    bucket: str
    key: str
    uri: str

    @property
    def name(self) -> str:
        return self.key.rstrip("/").rsplit("/", 1)[-1]


def parse_uri(uri: str) -> StorageRef:
    """Parse a storage URI (or a bare filesystem path) into a ``StorageRef``.

    A string with no ``scheme://`` prefix (including Windows ``C:\\...`` paths, which
    have no ``//``) is treated as a local filesystem path — preserving today's behaviour.
    """
    s = str(uri)
    m = _SCHEME_RE.match(s)
    if not m:
        return StorageRef("local", "", s, f"local://{s}")
    scheme = m.group(1).lower()
    rest = s[m.end():]
    if scheme == "gs":
        scheme = "gcs"
    if scheme in ("local", "gdrive"):
        # Path-keyed, no bucket: local FS, or a path under the mounted Google Drive "My Drive".
        return StorageRef(scheme, "", rest, s)
    if scheme in _KNOWN:
        bucket, _, key = rest.partition("/")
        return StorageRef(scheme, bucket, key, s)
    raise ValueError(f"unsupported storage URI scheme: {scheme}:// (in {s!r})")

Important question — let me answer it precisely, because the distinction matters for your alpha plan.

## Today: the cloud flow is **SSH/CLI-only**. There's **no UI trigger**, and **M12 doesn't change that.**

- **Correct** — to run highlight generation **on the cloud GPU today**, you (or anyone) must SSH into the VM and run the command (that's exactly what the dry-run guide is). You **cannot** share a page with alpha users for the cloud flow right now.
- **M12 won't fix this.** M12 is a *Google Drive storage backend* (point-at-Drive, write the reel back) — a storage **option**, not a UI. It adds zero UI trigger.
- **What does exist for UI:** a **local** flow — run the engine + the `web/` SPA on a machine, point at a video → reel → download (the prior session validated this end-to-end with Gemini). So one user on their own machine can use the UI today; it's just **not cloud-processing and not shareable**.

## What an alpha-shareable, UI-driven cloud flow actually needs (not just M12)

| Piece | State |
|---|---|
| Engine deployed as a **reachable hosted service** (Cloud Run service live) | ⛔ **M7 deploy** — the M6 *code* is done/mock-tested + the worker is GPU-proven (the 22-rally run), but it isn't *deployed as a persistent endpoint* yet |
| SPA **ingest/progress screen** (enter/upload video → `/api/process` → poll `/api/jobs` → load reel) | ⛔ khelsutra `web/` **B-P2** (net-new SPA work, no engine code) |
| **Edge auth ON** to gate alpha tenants | ✅ code merged today (M9, default-OFF) — flip on + set the CF config |
| **Compute picker in the UI** ("your machine vs our cloud") | ⛔ not built — the backend `deployment.profile` (M1) is the seam; the UI exposure is new |

So: **shareable-with-alpha = deploy M7 + build the SPA ingest screen + turn M9 on** — a deployment + frontend effort, gated on your GCP spend OK for a *persistent* service (vs the per-run VM/Job I've been deleting).
